# Iceberg lettuce

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="iceberg-lettuce-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="iceberg-lettuce-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="iceberg-lettuce-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Iceberg lettuce in the HMI library has 62 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Se, Ag, Au, Ba, Be, Mo, Sr, Th and Tl from 7 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Across 2 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported lead means and medians on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis ranged from 2 µg/kg (Martin-Leon et al. 2023) to 410 µg/kg (Guerra et al. 2012).<sup class="cite">[[sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Guerra F, Trevizam AR, Muraoka T, Marcante NC, Canniatti-Brazaca SG · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals">Heavy metals in vegetables and potential risk for human health</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientia Agricola 69(1):54-60</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Veronica Martin-Leon, Carmen Rubio, Angel Rodriguez-Hernandez, Manuel Zumbado, Andrea Acosta-Dacal, Luis Alberto Henriquez-Hernandez, Luis D. Luzardo · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements">Evaluation of essential, toxic and potentially toxic elements in leafy vegetables grown in the Canary Islands</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, 3 studies on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis reported means and medians from 4 µg/kg (Martin-Leon et al. 2023) to 34 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024).<sup class="cite">[[sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Veronica Martin-Leon, Carmen Rubio, Angel Rodriguez-Hernandez, Manuel Zumbado, Andrea Acosta-Dacal, Luis Alberto Henriquez-Hernandez, Luis D. Luzardo · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements">Evaluation of essential, toxic and potentially toxic elements in leafy vegetables grown in the Canary Islands</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the iceberg lettuce came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-iceberg-lettuce" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-iceberg-lettuce-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of iceberg lettuce, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

censoring. Each source drawer identifies methods and overlapping samples. No matched reference or pooled distribution is inferred.</p></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:iceberg-lettuce-a7e55691a4da:59b20acd5784"><summary>HMI iceberg lettuce Pb reference · Iceberg lettuce, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:iceberg-lettuce-a7e55691a4da:59b20acd5784"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI iceberg lettuce Pb reference · Iceberg lettuce, as sold. Iceberg lettuce, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤2.5</td><td>≤4</td><td>2–4 (bounded)</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤2.5</td><td>≤4</td><td>2–4 (bounded)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>46 reference units; 4 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>17</td><td>25%</td></tr><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>27</td><td>25%</td></tr><tr><td>Martin-Leon et al. 2023</td><td>1</td><td>25%</td></tr><tr><td>Spungen et al. 2024</td><td>1</td><td>25%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for iceberg lettuce, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>4 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li><li><a href="../sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements" class="internal" data-slug="sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements" data-no-cite-meta>Martin-Leon et al. 2023</a></li><li><a href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" class="internal" data-slug="sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" data-no-cite-meta>Spungen et al. 2024</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:iceberg-lettuce-a7e55691a4da:59b20acd5784</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:iceberg-lettuce-76f4d6f0f9db:4d4439516ee5"><summary>HMI iceberg lettuce Cd reference · Iceberg lettuce, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:iceberg-lettuce-76f4d6f0f9db:4d4439516ee5"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI iceberg lettuce Cd reference · Iceberg lettuce, as sold. GEMS/Food</a></p><p>Iceberg lettuce, as sold · Lead (Pb) · 88 results.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Selected finding and reference</p><div class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">median &lt;5 µg/kg (below reporting limits)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>88 results. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI iceberg lettuce Pb reference · Iceberg lettuce, as sold. median as reported: ≤5 µg/kg. HMI median ≤4; Q1 ≤2.5; Q3 2–4 (bounded) µg/kg. 4 families. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach iceberg lettuce

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared iceberg lettuce from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

The HMI reference for lead in iceberg lettuce, as sold pools 46 subgroup units from 4 sampling families, with a median of below 4 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 2.5 (lower quartile) and between 2 and 4 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/martin-leon2023-canary-leafy-vegetables-elements|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Veronica Martin-Leon, Carmen Rubio, Angel Rodriguez-Hernandez, Manuel Zumbado, Andrea Acosta-Dacal, Luis Alberto Henriquez-Hernandez, Luis D. <a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for iceberg lettuce

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing iceberg lettuce for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 4 µg/kg (FDA Total Diet Study) to 3,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 1 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 6.3 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Iceberg lettuce FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-iceberg-lettuce" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-iceberg-lettuce" data-question="What heavy metals are found in iceberg lettuce?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in iceberg lettuce?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Se, Ag, Au, Ba, Be, Mo, Sr, Th and Tl in iceberg lettuce, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span
